What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... they say I'm here because I spent our savings. Bought a lot of wood, lights, display stuff... I kept working on it after dark. I just... felt like if I stopped, the whole house, maybe the whole block, would lose its chance. That's what they say, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually energetic or irritable while working on your project?\n\nPatient: I suppose. Mostly I just... felt charged up. Good, you know? Like I was young again. But... well, people kept bothering me. If my wife, or... or the contractors started questioning what I was doing, I... I'd get hot fast. Might've started yelling. Just wanted them to leave me to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of feeling sad or empty in the past?\n\nPatient: I don't know. ... Not really, no. I just... I don't spend much time thinking about things like that. Just been busy, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like you didn't need to sleep while you were working on your project?\n\nPatient: I don't know. ... No, I don't think so. I still slept when I got tired. Just... didn't really pay much attention to it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like your project was especially important or that you were doing something that nobody else could do?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose. It's just that old house over in Schiller Park. I'm making it into something. Something people are gonna want to come see. I don't know... I just see things differently. Nobody else really understands it. The light, the pictures... not the way I do. So... yeah. Had to be me, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were somehow special or had a special mission while working on your project?\n\nPatient: I don't know about all that. ... A mission? No. It ain't nothing like that. Just... doing what needed doing, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were better than others or more important than you normally feel while working on your project?\n\nPatient: No. Not better. Just... like I said, I was the one who could see how it ought to be. That's all. Didn't make me better than nobody. Just... working.\n\nDoctor: Did your spending and project work happen all at once, or was it spread out over a few weeks?\n\nPatient: It wasn't all at once. ... Been about three months, I reckon. Just... ever since the remodeling started. Everything in the house got turned upside down. So... yeah. Spread out.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like your project was going to change the world or have a significant impact on a lot of people?\n\nPatient: Change the world? No. I don't know about all that. I just... I believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something important. Something folks are gonna want to come see. Because... well, nobody really understands light and pictures the way I do. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel normal or back to your usual self during those three months while working on your project?\n\nPatient: I don't rightly know. ... Just felt like me, I guess. I was busy. Didn't really have time to sit around thinking about how I felt.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt sad or empty for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Sad? I don't know. ... Can't say that I have. Just been busy with the house.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you had any special powers or abilities while working on your project?\n\nPatient: Powers? No. ... I ain't talking about magic or nothing. Like I told you a few minutes ago, it's just... I honestly believe I can make that old Schiller Park house into something important. Something people will come look at. It's just because nobody else understands light and pictures the way I do. That ain't a special power. It's just how it is.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like you weren't charged up or excited about your project at any point during those three months?\n\nPatient: Not during those three months, no. I was just working. \n\nI mean... before I got so charged up with all the remodeling plans, I wasn't noticing things. Like the trucks on the street, or the way the neighbors were looking. I didn't see no special meaning in none of that back then. \n\nThose signs... they only really started showing up once I felt driven to turn the place into my big civic project. But once I started on it, I didn't slow down.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Something people will come look at. It's just because nobody else understands light and pictures the way I do. That ain't a special power. It's just how it is.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like you weren't charged up or excited about your project at any point during those three months?\n\nPatient: Not during those three months, no. I was just working. \n\nI mean... before I got so charged up with all the remodeling plans, I wasn't noticing things. Like the trucks on the street, or the way the neighbors were looking. I didn't see no special meaning in none of that back then. \n\nThose signs... they only really started showing up once I felt driven to turn the place into my big civic project. Like I said, I was just... I kept working. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any periods where you felt extremely high or euphoric that lasted for several days or more?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah. I still slept when I got tired. I suppose. It's just that old house over in Schiller Park. I'm making it into something. Something people are gonna want to come see. I don't know... I just see things differently. Had to be me, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were somehow special or had a special mission while working on your project?\n\nPatient: I don't know about all that. ... A mission? It's just how it is.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like you weren't charged up or excited about your project at any point during those three months?\n\nPatient: Not during those three months, no. Just wanted them to leave me to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of feeling sad or empty in the past?\n\nPatient: I don't know. ... Not really, no. I just... I don't spend much time thinking about things like that. Just been busy, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like you didn't need to sleep while you were working on your project?\n\nPatient: I don't know. ... No, I don't think so. I still slept when I got tired. Just... didn't really pay much attention to it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like your project was especially important or that you were doing something that nobody else could do?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose. It's just that old house over in Schiller Park. I'm making it into something. Something people are gonna want to come see. I don't know... I just see things differently. Nobody else really understands it. The light, the pictures... not the way I do. So... yeah. Had to be me, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were somehow special or had a special mission while working on your project?\n\nPatient: I don't know about all that. ... A mission? No. It ain't nothing like that. Just... doing what needed doing, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were better than others or more important than you normally feel while working on your project?\n\nPatient: No. Not better. Just... like I said, I was the one who could see how it ought to be. That's all. Didn't make me better than nobody. Just... working.\n\nDoctor: Did your spending and project work happen all at once, or was it spread out over a few weeks?\n\nPatient: It wasn't all at once. ... Been about three months, I reckon. Just... ever since the remodeling started. Everything in the house got turned upside down. So... yeah. Spread out.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like your project was going to change the world or have a significant impact on a lot of people?\n\nPatient: Change the world? No. I don't know about all that. I just... I believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something important. Something folks are gonna want to come see. Because... well, nobody really understands light and pictures the way I do. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel normal or back to your usual self during those three months while working on your project?\n\nPatient: I don't rightly know. ... Just felt like me, I guess. I was busy. Didn't really have time to sit around thinking about how I felt.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt sad or empty for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Sad? I don't know. ... Can't say that I have. Just been busy with the house.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you had any special powers or abilities while working on your project?\n\nPatient: Powers? No. ... I ain't talking about magic or nothing. Like I told you a few minutes ago, it's just... I honestly believe I can make that old Schiller Park house into something important. Something people will come look at. It's just because nobody else understands light and pictures the way I do. That ain't a special power. It's just how it is.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like you weren't charged up or excited about your project at any point during those three months?\n\nPatient: Not during those three months, no. I was just working. \n\nI mean... before I got so charged up with all the remodeling plans, I wasn't noticing things. Like the trucks on the street, or the way the neighbors were looking. I didn't see no special meaning in none of that back then. \n\nThose signs... they only really started showing up once I felt driven to turn the place into my big civic project. But once I started on it, I didn't slow down.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like you weren't charged up or excited about your project at any point during those three months?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't. Like I said, I was just... I kept working. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any periods where you felt extremely high or euphoric that lasted for several days or more?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah. Most days, I feel pretty charged up. Almost joyful, really. Like I'm young again. But... I don't know. It's just, if my wife or those contractors start questioning what I'm doing, I... I get hot fast. I start yelling. I just want them to let me work.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time during those three months when you didn't feel charged up or excited, even for just a part of the day?\n\nPatient: I... I thought I just answered that. No. Not even for part of a day. I don't remember feeling any different, I just kept going.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you felt sad or empty for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: No. Not that I can think of. I just... I haven't felt like that.\n\nDoctor: Did your feeling of being charged up and joyful come and go, or was it there all the time during those three months?\n\nPatient: I... I just said that, didn't I? It didn't come and go. It was just there. The whole time.\n\nDoctor: Did your period of feeling charged up and joyful last for at least four consecutive days?\n\nPatient: Yes. Like I said... it was three months. So... yes, it was more than four days. I don't... I don't really get why we're still on this.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time during those three months when you felt your usual self, not charged up or excited?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. I... it just stayed like that the whole time. I didn't go back to normal.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
